In November 2024, mass protests began in [Novi Sad](113288.md) after the [city's railway station canopy collapsed](78262640.md), killing 16 people and leaving one severely injured. By March 2025, student-led protests calling for accountability spread to 400 cities and towns across [Serbia](29265.md).[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66]

The protests began with student-led blockades of educational institutions, initiated on 22 November at the [Faculty of Dramatic Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faculty_of_Dramatic_Arts) in [Belgrade](55904.md), after students were attacked during a silent tribute to the victims of the 1 November collapse. Other university faculties and high schools soon joined. Protesters enacted daily *"Serbia, stand still*" ([Serbian Cyrillic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Cyrillic_alphabet): Застани, Србијо, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Serbian): *Zastani, Srbijo*) [traffic blockades](53267635.md) from 11\:52 am to 12\:08 pm—the exact time of the collapse—as a symbolic gesture honoring the 16 lives lost, accompanied by a [silent protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silent_protest).[^cite_note-67]

By early 2025, the movement transitioned into sustained civil disobedience. Protesters began organizing extended road blockades, walking demonstrations, a protest cycling event to [Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg), a relay race to [Brussels](3708.md), and blockades of the headquarters of [Radio Television of Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Television_of_Serbia). Confrontations escalated as supporters of the ruling [Serbian Progressive Party](19454384.md) (SNS) and the police clashed with demonstrators, leading to street fights and arrests in multiple cities like [Novi Pazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Pazar) and [Užice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C5%BEice).

After several months of student [lockouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockout_\(industry\)) within most universities in Serbia, including the [University of Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Belgrade) and the [University of Novi Sad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Novi_Sad), the 2025/2026 school year has commenced in all previously locked-out institutions,[^cite_note-68] despite continuing [anti-academic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-intellectualism) rhetoric. As of December 2025, Vučić's government has continued to falsely assert that lockouts have continued.[^cite_note-69]

## Background

Main article: [Novi Sad railway station canopy collapse](78262640.md)

On 1 November 2024, the canopy of the [Novi Sad railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Sad_railway_station) collapsed, initially killing 14 people and leaving three with injuries; one of whom died on 17 November and another on 21 March 2025.[^cite_note-70] The collapse led to widespread concern in Serbia, with many questioning the structural integrity and maintenance oversight of public infrastructure. Local authorities launched an investigation into the causes of the incident, but frustration grew due to a lack of accountability in the response.[^cite_note-71] The station building was constructed in 1964 and was renovated from 2021 to mid-2024 with support from [China](5405.md)'s [Belt and Road Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belt_and_Road_Initiative). The official cause of the collapse remains under investigation, with government corruption and non-transparent [dealings with Chinese contractors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Serbia_relations#Economic_relations) blamed.[^cite_note-72]

## Protests

### Early post-disaster dissent

Early protests primarily took the form of quiet vigils for victims of the collapse.[^cite_note-AP_News-2024a-73] These protests became larger and more visible demonstrations over time, with protesters accusing police and local authorities of negligence and [corruption](42552633.md). Protesters began demanding a transparent investigation into the collapse and the release of documents related to the incident. The [Associated Press](18935732.md) suggested that the collapse also served as a flashpoint for expressions of dissatisfaction with the Serbian government as a whole.[^cite_note-AP_News-2024a-73]

President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md), Minister of Construction [Goran Vesić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goran_Vesi%C4%87) and pro-government news outlets initially claimed the canopy was never reconstructed, despite videos and images posted online that showed Vesić opening the reconstructed Novi Sad Railway Station.[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76] Faced with accusations and public outrage, Vesić announced his resignation, but stated that he "does not feel responsible".[^cite_note-77]

Some initial demonstrations escalated to vandalism, with the [Novi Sad City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Sad_City_Hall) being a primary target.[^cite_note-78] Red paint was thrown on the city hall entrance and attempts were made to breach the building.[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81] Law enforcement responded with tear gas and arrests, further inflaming tensions. Protesters suggested that these and other violent demonstrations were the result of government plants seeking to derail the protests.

### Initial political fallout and arrests

On 20 November, the Minister of Trade [Tomislav Momirović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomislav_Momirovi%C4%87) resigned from his position due to the protests. In his resignation letter, he pledged his "eternal loyalty to president Vučić".[^cite_note-82]

On 21 November, [Vladimir Đukanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87), a high-ranking member of the ruling [Serbian Progressive Party](19454384.md) (SNS), commented negatively on the protests on his [Twitter](9988187.md) account, stating that "\[w]e must fight against anarcho-terrorists, fake commie intellectuals, \[and] the pseudo-elite that is ravaging Serbia with anti-Serbian attitudes. It is time to stop this social scum. In every place and at every step. First of all, in every discussion, and God forbid, if necessary, with force. This scum will no longer be able to terrorize this country. Long live Serbia and just fight bravely".[^cite_note-83] On the same day, former Minister Vesić was arrested along with 11 unspecified persons.[^cite_note-84] He was released after six days and kept his party rank within the SNS.[^cite_note-Blic-Goran-6]

### Blockade of the Faculty of Dramatic Arts and growth of the protests

On 22 November, students and professors of the Faculty of Dramatic Arts gathered in the immediate vicinity of the Faculty to pay homage to the fatalities in Novi Sad. The meeting was announced to the authorities in accordance with the law. At the gathering, both students and professors were attacked by an organized group.[^cite_note-Milovanović-2024-85] Some of the attackers were allegedly high-ranking officials of the SNS.[^cite_note-Milovanović-2024-85] After the attack on 25 November, the students began an occupation of the faculty in protest. They were soon joined by the Faculty of Philosophy in Novi Sad, and the [Faculty of Philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faculty_of_Philosophy,_University_of_Belgrade), the [Faculty of Philology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Belgrade_Faculty_of_Philology), the Faculty of Natural Sciences and Mathematics, and the [Faculty of Political Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faculty_of_Political_Sciences,_University_of_Belgrade) in Belgrade.[^cite_note-86]

A November demonstration in Novi Sad drew 20,000 protesters, making it the largest protest within Novi Sad in decades.[^cite_note-Delauney-2024-87] Demonstrators held weekly 15-minute traffic blockades on Fridays at 11\:52 am, the time of the collapse.[^cite_note-88] Cars occasionally rammed protesters during these blockades.[^cite_note-Gec-2024-89][^cite_note-AP_News-2024b-90]

On 22 November, the students and citizens of [Leskovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leskovac), in southern Serbia, organized a protest. It started with 15 minutes of silence in honor of the victims of the canopy collapse. The protest was attended by the mayor of Leskovac, Goran Cvetanović of SNS.[^cite_note-91] The mayor later retracted his support, calling the protesters "students with bad grades" and shared their school grades with the public, in violation of Serbian data protection laws.[^cite_note-92]

In late November, an audio recording was leaked to the press of the alleged voice of [Vojvodina assemblyman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assembly_of_Vojvodina) [Damir Zobenica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damir_Zobenica) advising other party members to insult and attack the protesters blocking the roads. Zobenica resigned after the leak.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94]

On 1 December, a silent protest was held in Novi Sad to commemorate one month since the collapse.[^cite_note-95] On the same day, President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md) publicly announced that those who asked for the arrest of the car driver who intentionally rammed into the protesters were "out of their minds", because "the driver was just going on his way".[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97]

### Spread of the blockading tactic and first concessions



Campuses in Belgrade blocked by students, left to right: School of Electrical Engineering, Faculty of Law, Faculty of Philosophy, and Faculty of Fine Arts.

By early December, Serbian students had begun organizing 24-hour blockades at some school campuses.[^cite_note-AP_News-2024b-90] By mid-December, more than 50 university campuses (including the three biggest universities of [Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Belgrade), [Novi Sad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Novi_Sad) and [Niš](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Ni%C5%A1)) and multiple secondary schools had suspended classes due to student protests.[^cite_note-Gec-2024-89]

On 5 December, the Bar Association of Serbia announced that affilated lawyers would organize a one-day strike on 11 December because of "systematic and long-term interference by the executive branch in the work of the judiciary branch and violation of the principle of separation of powers in a democratic society", along with the lack of accountability in the Novi Sad collapse investigations.[^cite_note-98]

On 6 December, during a 15-minute blockade in downtown Belgrade, a car rammed into the crowd, injuring four members of the [Belgrade Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgrade_Philharmonic_Orchestra) that were protesting.[^cite_note-99] The Orchestra condemned the attack and the driver was arrested.[^cite_note-100]

On 11 December, students demonstrated at the headquarters of the public television station [RTS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTS_2_\(Serbian_TV_channel\)), for their broadcasting of President Aleksandar Vučić's claims that demonstrators are being funded by Western countries who seek to destabilize Serbia.[^cite_note-101][^cite_note-Gec-2024-89][^cite_note-102] The same day, Vučić promised that all prosecutorial documents related to the disaster would be publicized, announced that all protesters held in jail were released, and pledged to pardon any protesters if they were convicted at trial. While the concessions marked the first time since Vučić's rise to power in 2012 that any concessions were made to protesters, they did not include the protest's calls for his resignation.[^cite_note-103]

On 12 December, in [Čačak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%8Ca%C4%8Dak), students of the Faculty of Technical Sciences and the Agronomic Faculty organized a protest, which began with 15 minutes of silence.[^cite_note-104]

### Widening mobilization

On 13 December, farmers in central Serbia blocked [Ibar Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibar_Highway) with tractors.[^cite_note-Gec-2024-89][^cite_note-105] That same day, in Novi Sad, a group of four [hooligans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hooliganism) attempted to ram their [Porsche Cayenne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porsche_Cayenne) vehicle and physically attacked the protesters.[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] The attackers were later arrested; one was revealed to be an active duty police officer.[^cite_note-108]

On 15 December, in a TV interview, Prime Minister Miloš Vučević said that "you can't bring down a country because of 15 people who died, nor 155, nor 1,555".[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110] Vučević later apologized for his remarks.

On 17 December, high school students in [Mladenovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mladenovac) commemorated the victims of the canopy collapse with 15 minutes of silence.[^cite_note-111]

On 19 December, commenting on the involvement of high school students in the protests, [Vladimir Đukanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87), a high-ranking official of the SNS, stated that "children are property of the state until they reach the [age of majority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_majority)".[^cite_note-112] The statement generated controversy,[^cite_note-113] with the Commissioner for the Protection of Equality, Brankica Janković, describing his remarks as "utterly unfounded, dangerous, and condemnable".[^cite_note-114] Đukanović defended his statement by claiming it was "metaphorical" and "taken out of context".[^cite_note-115]

On 20 December, the silent protest was extended by one minute to commemorate a victim of the [Zagreb school stabbing](78646529.md) in Croatia.[^cite_note-116]

### First Slavija protest

In response to [police brutality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_brutality) and alleged paid [hooligans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hooliganism) who attacked civilians and protesters, opposition leaders, students, farmers and independent demonstrators organized a large-scale protest on 22 December, at [Slavija Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavija_Square) in Belgrade.[^cite_note-117] The estimated attendance was between 100,000 and 102,000 people, which marks the biggest protest in Belgrade and Serbia by attendance in history.[^cite_note-118]

In an 24 December interview, Vučić stated that "if \[he] wished so, \[he] could send out the [Cobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cobras_\(Serbia\)) on the students and they would throw them around in 6–7 seconds".[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] This statement was widely ridiculed, causing the students to make [memes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memes) making fun of Vučić and his phrase "throwing around" ("razbacati"). The Cobras do not have the authority to disperse crowds.[^cite_note-121]

On 25 December, people brought 1,000 letters to the office of the public prosecutor [Zagorka Dolovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zagorka_Dolovac?action=edit\&redlink=1) urging her to "start doing her job".[^cite_note-122] That same day, employees of the Serbian intelligence agency [BIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Intelligence_Agency) paid an unofficial visit to the mother of one of the student organizers at her workplace. She interpreted this move as a "threat" and as "pressure".[^cite_note-123] Other organizers allegedly received telephone calls inviting them to come to BIA offices "for a friendly chat", but no official summons were handed.[^cite_note-124] In the evening, over 2,000 people protested in [Užice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C5%BEice), in front of the city hall.[^cite_note-125] The local authority, staffed by SNS members, turned off the street lights in front of the hall, but were turned on when the protestors brought a portable [power generator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_generator).[^cite_note-126]

### December incidents and the announcement of the "loyalist faction"

On 27 December, protesters blocked a road in [Ivanjica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivanjica), causing a driver to ram his car into the crowd. Once his vehicle stopped, the gathered protesters attacked him, however he was immediately protected by undercover police standing nearby and allowed to leave the area without legal repercussions.[^cite_note-127] That same day, 200 employees of RTS signed a petition in support of the protests.[^cite_note-128]

On 29 December, Vučić publicly accused eight Croatian students from [FER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faculty_of_Electrical_Engineering_and_Computing,_University_of_Zagreb), who were visiting their fellow students in Belgrade, of organizing the protests on behalf of the Croatian intelligence agency [SOA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_and_Intelligence_Agency_\(Croatia\)). The names of the Croatian students were published without their consent in the pro-government media and, upon leaving Serbia, they were given alcoholic beverages with a note to "send their regards to SOA" by the Serbian border police.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] Croatian prime minister [Andrej Plenković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Plenkovi%C4%87) described Vučić's claim as "laughable".[^cite_note-131]

On 31 December, Vučić announced the formation of a "loyalist faction" within the SNS, whose "17,000 members swore a blood oath in secrecy", who are "[pro-Russian](2541825.md) oriented" and are "a bit too extreme for \[his] taste". He also revealed that his brother is among the members.[^cite_note-Direktno-2024-132][^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134] It is unclear whether this was directed towards the protesters or the more reluctant members of SNS,[^cite_note-135] whereas others expressed doubt that Vučić could have gathered 17,000 loyal activists.[^cite_note-136] A group of citizens signed a petition demanding an official police investigation into the existence of these loyalists.[^cite_note-137]

Similar to the [1996–1997 Serbian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996%E2%80%931997_Serbian_protests), silent protests in Belgrade and Novi Sad were held on New Year's Eve, but this time from 11\:52 pm to 00\:07 am. According to some estimations, between 17,000 and 18,000 attended in Belgrade, while between 4,600 and 6,000 attended in Novi Sad.[^cite_note-138]

On 2 January, the 15 minutes of silence were extended to 29: an additional 12 for the people killed in [2025 Cetinje shootings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Cetinje_shootings) in Montenegro on 31 December, and two for the victims killed in [Arilje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arilje) on 1 January.[^cite_note-139] The action was repeated on 3 January in Novi Sad at the blockade of the [Varadin Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varadin_Bridge) and in other cities.[^cite_note-140]

### Early mobilization of education workers

On 3 January, a student protest was held in [Aleksandrovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandrovac), the largest in the town's history. The students were joined by other citizens, including some of their professors.[^cite_note-141] On the same day, after a round of negotiations with education workers' unions, Prime Minister Vučević stated that all education workers who decide to strike will be fired and replaced.[^cite_note-142] This statement caused widespread negative reactions among education workers, who characterized the statement as "arrogant" and "insulting".[^cite_note-143]

The government tabloid *[Večernje Novosti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ve%C4%8Dernje_Novosti)* published pictures of Croatian passports showing the names and personal data of two student protesters, claiming they are "Croatian spies" sent to "destabilize Serbia". In reality, the students in question came from [Croatian Serb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Serb) [refugee](45547.md) families and held both Serbian and Croatian citizenship. The students announced they will sue *Večernje Novosti*, due to a breach of the Serbian data protection laws. The public widely condemned the article.[^cite_note-144]

### January upsurge of nationwide protests

On 6 January, high-school students in [Požarevac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Po%C5%BEarevac) organized a protest, which began with 15 minutes of silence.[^cite_note-145]

On 10 January, protesters in Belgrade gathered in front of the Palace of Justice, and afterwards blocked the ring road in downtown Belgrade. Protests were also held in Novi Sad, Niš, [Zrenjanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zrenjanin) and [Kikinda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kikinda).[^cite_note-146]

On 11 January, protesters gathered in front of the offices of [BIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Intelligence_Agency) in Novi Sad, inviting the BIA employees over "for a friendly chat". The building was heavily guarded by police.[^cite_note-147] A large protest was simultaneously organized in [Kragujevac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kragujevac) by local high-school students, joined by medical workers.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] During the night, students of technical faculties in Belgrade reported that the unknown persons entered the campus despite the blockade and started threatening them.[^cite_note-Nova-2025b-150]

On 12 January, around 20,000 students and citizens gathered in front of the [Constitutional Court of Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Serbia) in Belgrade, demanding the prosecution of those responsible for the canopy collapse and expressing their support to the school workers' union which announced a possible [general strike](41138909.md). The protest began with 15 minutes of silence.[^cite_note-Nova-2025b-150][^cite_note-151] At the same time, students in Niš organized a protest in front of the Rectorate of the [University of Niš](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Ni%C5%A1). They put up a banner saying "Work stoppage due to protest" on the second floor of the Rectorate building.[^cite_note-152]

On 13 January, late in the evening, unknown persons physically attacked the students blocking the Belgrade Faculty of Law, causing six students to be injured. The police intervened and one of the attackers was arrested.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154]

### Continued mobilization of education workers

On 14 January, the largest education workers' union (NSPRS) announced a one-day [general strike](41138909.md), displeased with the government policy towards teachers and professors. The union representatives officially expressed their support for students and announced they will join the protests.[^cite_note-155] On the same day, the workers union of [Elektroprivreda Srbije](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elektroprivreda_Srbije), Serbia's state-owned power company, announced a general strike as well.[^cite_note-156] In downtown Belgrade, thousands of students marched in a rally, expressing their support for the assaulted law students and the general strike.[^cite_note-157]

On 16 January, during a 15-minute blockade in downtown Belgrade, a dark blue [Ford Focus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Focus) vehicle drove through the protesting students, carrying one student on the car roof for several meters without stopping.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159] The injured student was taken to hospital and the driver was arrested and charged with attempted murder.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161] This incident caused widespread outrage, causing thousands of people to rally later in the day in Belgrade, Novi Sad, Zrenjanin, Kragujevac and [Kraljevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraljevo); whereas in Niš, some of the faculty professors joined the student blockade.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165] In response to the protests in Kraljevo, the mayor of Kraljevo, Predrag Terzić, member of the SNS, compared the protestors with [Ustaše](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usta%C5%A1e) members and published their names without their consent.[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167]

#### 16 January Čačak incident

On 16 January, in Čačak, [hooligans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hooliganism) stormed the family home of a [religious teacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_education_in_primary_and_secondary_education) who was a union representative, threatening him into voting against the next general strike. Earlier during the day, the teacher met with mayor Milun Todorović, where he was asked to vote against the strike.[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169] This sparked outrage throughout Čačak, and during the next day, thousands gathered in support of the teacher.[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171]

### Attempted occupation of the City Assembly of Novi Sad

On 17 January, opposition members of the Novi Sad city assembly took control of the Novi Sad city hall, but were soon forced out by the police in [riot gear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riot_gear).[^cite_note-172] Later during the day, an older man carrying a [pocket knife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pocket_knife) tried to attack the students protesting in front of the Medical Faculty in Novi Sad.[^cite_note-173] On the same day, protesters gathered in [Vranje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vranje), where the names of 15 victims of the canopy collapse were read out loud.[^cite_note-174]

### Proclamation of the four demands



RTS employees publicly express their support for students, during the protest on 17 January.

On the evening of 17 January, tens of thousands of students and citizens organized a large protest in front of the RTS building in Belgrade due to dissatisfaction with the way RTS covered the protests, once comparing the protesters' symbols with that of [Hamas](13913.md).[^cite_note-175] The protest began with 15 minutes of silence. Afterwards, the students paraded with a giant Cobra doll, parodying Vučić's earlier statements about Cobra special forces, and with a giant sandwich intended for the CEO of RTS, parodying the cliché of SNS supporters being paid with sandwiches.[^cite_note-176] The protesters played the audio recording of their demands:

1. the publication of the entire documentation on the reconstruction of the Railway Station,

2. the dismissal of the charges against the arrested and detained students, activists and citizens at the previous protests regarding the tragedy in Novi Sad,

3. the filing of criminal charges against all attackers of students, professors and citizens,

4. an increase in the budget for faculties for 20 percent.

   [^cite_note-177]

The workers of the RTS gathered on the balcony and showed the banners saying "RTS employees support the students" and "You cannot run us over", referencing the vehicular attacks on the protestors.[^cite_note-178]

On 18 January, the Bar Association of Serbia announced a 7-day general strike, commencing on 20 January. During that period, the lawyers will not attend any court proceedings.[^cite_note-179] When asked about the lawyer's strike in a TV interview, Vučić compared lawyers to "a mob".[^cite_note-180] The Bar Association condemned Vučić's statement and announced a protest.[^cite_note-181] On the same day, teachers and professors protested in Novi Sad, demanding the resignation of the university rector, and accusing him of supporting the attacks on students.[^cite_note-182] Education workers also protested in [Pirot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pirot).[^cite_note-183] Students, school teachers and citizens protested in [Kragujevac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kragujevac), as well.[^cite_note-184] Students announced that a blockade of all three bridges in Novi Sad ([Liberty Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Bridge,_Novi_Sad), [Varadin Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varadin_Bridge) and [Žeželj Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDe%C5%BEelj_Bridge)) will take place on 1 February, to mark the three months since the canopy collapse while also being the Day of the city.[^cite_note-185]

### Intensified education sector dissent

On 19 January, around 10,000 teachers, professors, students and citizens protested in [Čačak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%8Ca%C4%8Dak) in support of the attacked religious teacher. Students carried a cardboard replica of a bulldozer, as a reference to the [Bulldozer Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulldozer_Revolution). In front of the SNS offices, the protesters stopped and shouted "[fascists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism), fascists", "resignations" and threw eggs at the windows.[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187] In Kraljevo, mayor Predrag Terzić was [booed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booed) in public because of his posts against the student protesters.[^cite_note-188] In Belgrade, 20,000 education workers protested and were joined by students. The protest symbolically lasted for 45 minutes.[^cite_note-189]

Due to the existence of several different education workers' unions, the decision on the strike was ultimately left to individual schools. Around 35 percent of schools, both elementary and high schools throughout Serbia, decided to go on strike starting 20 January.[^cite_note-190] In [Subotica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subotica), students and citizens protested in the city center, expressing their support for lawyers and education workers. This protest also began with 15 minutes of silence.[^cite_note-191]

On 20 January, parents gathered in front of several elementary schools in Novi Sad to express their support for the teachers.[^cite_note-192] In the Belgrade municipality of [Surčin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sur%C4%8Din), dozens of hooded people, allegedly tied to SNS, tried to forcefully enter an elementary school which was on strike, and verbally threatened the principal and teachers. Some parents came to assist the teachers, filmed the attackers, and the attackers eventually left.[^cite_note-193] In [Zrenjanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zrenjanin), school inspectors were ordered by the Ministry of Education to enter the elementary schools which declared strike, but they refused.[^cite_note-194]

In some schools in Belgrade and Novi Sad, parents gathered and chased out the school inspectors.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] Students protested in [Novi Pazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Pazar). The silence was extended to 16 minutes, in protest against the lenient sentence for the for the driver who killed Ernad Bakan in a traffic accident [^cite_note-Vreme-Novi_Pazar-197][^cite_note-198] Employees of [Elektroprivreda Srbije](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elektroprivreda_Srbije) also declared a strike and announced they will be joining the protests.[^cite_note-199]

### Color revolution allegation

On 21 January, hooded people put up banners on the overpasses on the Belgrade highway, showing a picture of a [flipped middle finger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_finger) with the inscription "This is the response of the Serbian people to your [colour revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colour_revolution)".[^cite_note-200] Many prominent SNS members, including the mayor of Kraljevo, also shared the picture of the flipped middle finger on their social media profiles.[^cite_note-201] The middle finger was also drawn overnight in several Belgrade schoolyards, but pupils drew flowers over them in response.[^cite_note-202] It is worth noting that term "colour revolution", although common in countries of the [former Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_Soviet_Union), was never widely used in Serbia. The activists would later take down the banners. In one case, a hooded person who helped put up the banners attacked the activists; the attacker was filmed and [doxxed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxxed) later.[^cite_note-203] On the same day, high-school students and citizens protested in [Obrenovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obrenovac).[^cite_note-204]

In the evening hours of 21 January, undercover police stormed the Belgrade hotel where students from abroad were staying for an international conference, organized by [ERSTE Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ERSTE_Foundation). Five female students from Croatia were singled out by police, transported to the police station, accused of helping the protests and deported. This act was condemned by Croatia, as well as by anti-government protesters in Serbia.[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207]

### January 24 general strike

#### Prelude

By 22 January, dozens of companies throughout Serbia announced that they will perform a [general strike](41138909.md) on 24 January. Some online retail websites were temporarily taken offline between 11\:52 am and 12\:07 pm. Employees of [technology companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technology_companies) headquartered in [New Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Belgrade) joined the students in road blockades and protests. In Čačak, medical workers joined the protest.[^cite_note-208] That same day, two MPs from the SNS officially resigned, without providing an explanation.[^cite_note-209]

On 23 January, during the protest in Kraljevo, protesters threw eggs at the local SNS offices and at the mayor, Predrag Terzić, who was present in front of the offices. Protesters shouted "murderers, murderers".[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] On that same day, in an open letter, 17 judges publicly expressed their support for students' demands.[^cite_note-212] Over a hundred different companies publicly stated they will support the general strike and will not operate on 24 January.[^cite_note-213]

#### Events



A march during the general strike in Belgrade on 24 January 2025. The banner in the foreground reads "**С**амо **С**тудент **С**рбина **С**пашава" (Only a Student\[s] Saves the Serbs), a play on the national slogan "[Only Unity Saves the Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Only_Unity_Saves_the_Serbs)".

On 24 January, large protests were held in [Apatin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apatin), Belgrade, [Gornji Milanovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gornji_Milanovac), [Jagodina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagodina), [Lazarevac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lazarevac), Leskovac, Kragujevac, Niš, Novi Sad, Novi Pazar, [Petrovac na Mlavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrovac_na_Mlavi), [Stara Pazova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stara_Pazova), and [Vlasotince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlasotince).[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] In Lazarevac, the students were joined by miners from the [RB Kolubara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RB_Kolubara) mines. In Belgrade, high-school pupils, lawyers and tech companies organized their protest together with students. Two columns of protesters crossed the [Branko's Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branko's_Bridge) from both sides and symbolically connected two sides of the river.[^cite_note-217] Medical workers and workers in social care institutions either left their workplaces while the protesters were passing by or made statements of support.[^cite_note-218] In Novi Sad, opposition parties blocked the highway with motor vehicles for a brief period of time.[^cite_note-219]

### Jagodina SNS rally

The protest in Jagodina was specific since it was organized on the same day as the large SNS gathering, albeit a few hours earlier. Despite that, there were no reported incidents within the city; however, in the Belgrade municipality of [Voždovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vo%C5%BEdovac), local residents attempted to physically stop a bus driving SNS supporters to Jagodina.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] The SNS supporters arrived later in the afternoon.

Speaking at the SNS gathering, president Vučić stated that he wants peace and stability, that "children need to go to schools", and that he will "not sanction [Russia](25391.md), nor any other friendly nation". He also announced the formation of a new political movement.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224]

### Autokomanda blockade

During the protests in [New Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Belgrade), a black [Citroën](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citro%C3%ABn) ran over two students and seriously injured them. Afterwards, the protesters surrounded the vehicle and destroyed it. The driver, a young woman, was arrested by undercover police.[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226] Students announced that they will organize a day-long blockade of the [Autokomanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autokomanda) intersection in downtown Belgrade on 27 January.[^cite_note-227] The Ministry of Education called on schools to reduce the January salaries for those education workers who are on strike.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]

In the morning hours of 25 January, over 650 [bikers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorcycling) from different biker clubs gathered in Belgrade, donated food to students blocking the Faculty of Dramatic Arts, and afterwards proceeded downtown, in support of the student who was overrun. Their protest began with 15 minutes of silence, honoring the victims of the canopy collapse.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] Farmers from towns and villages around Novi Sad formed columns and moved towards the city with their tractors. Their proclaimed goal was to park the tractors in front of protesting students, thereby shielding them from possible attacks. Near [Rumenka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rumenka), the farmers were blocked by a unit of [Gendarmery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gendarmery_\(Serbia\)) with riot gear, prohibiting them from moving further. Students from Novi Sad came to assist the farmers and the unit was eventually forced to pull back. Around 30 tractors reached the campus in Novi Sad.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] Citizens also protested in [Mladenovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mladenovac),[^cite_note-234] [Pirot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pirot),[^cite_note-235] [Prijepolje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prijepolje),[^cite_note-236] [Smederevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smederevo),[^cite_note-237] [Svrljig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svrljig),[^cite_note-238] [Vršac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vr%C5%A1ac)[^cite_note-239] and [Zrenjanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zrenjanin).[^cite_note-240]

On the morning of 26 January, farmers from [Banatsko Novo Selo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banatsko_Novo_Selo) moved towards Belgrade with their tractors. Their aim was to supply food for students blocking the Belgrade Agronomic Faculty and to leave at least 10 tractors there as a shield against possible attacks.[^cite_note-241] The farmers reached the faculty in the afternoon.[^cite_note-242]

On 26 January, bikers organized a protest in Kragujevac, commemorating the victims with 15 minutes of silence.[^cite_note-243] Citizens also protested in Niš,[^cite_note-244] Subotica,[^cite_note-245] [Varvarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varvarin)[^cite_note-246] and [Zaječar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaje%C4%8Dar).[^cite_note-247]

On the morning of 27 January, students, bikers and farmers on tractors blocked the [Autokomanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autokomanda) intersection in downtown Belgrade. The blockade began with 15 minutes of silence and was scheduled to last for 24 hours.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] Tens of thousands attended the blockade; students played volleyball and boardgames, and made barbecue. Early the next day, the students cleaned up the intersection.[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251] At the same time, citizens protested in [Prokuplje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prokuplje) and [Kuršumlija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kur%C5%A1umlija).[^cite_note-252] Civil engineers announced that they would protest on 29 January. They also asked the Ministry of Construction to amend the Law on Planning and Construction and appoint experts to key positions in the relevant institutions.[^cite_note-253]

### Novi Sad baseball bat attack and Vučević's resignation

Around 3 a.m. on 28 January, students putting up posters in Novi Sad were attacked by masked individuals carrying baseball bats in front of the local offices of SNS. After repeated calls and 30 minutes of waiting for the emergency medical team to arrive, students were transported to the hospital.[^cite_note-254] One student was seriously injured and left with a broken jaw.[^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256] This caused an outrage on [social media](5897742.md). In response, the students announced a new protest in Novi Sad. Around 10 am, students gathered and drew [graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graffiti) on the windows of SNS offices in Novi Sad.[^cite_note-257] At a press conference held at 11 am, Prime Minister Miloš Vučević, and mayor of Novi Sad Milan Đurić, both members of SNS, resigned, citing the attack as the reason. Vučević also informed the public that the attackers indeed came from the offices of the SNS.[^cite_note-258] Four attackers were arrested. Every attacker had a previous criminal record.[^cite_note-259] They were soon [doxxed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxxed) and had their mugshots from the police station were leaked on social media. Some social media users found several old photos of Vučević and his son together with some of the attackers; despite rumors of this being a possible reason for resignation, his alleged personal connection with the attackers was not officially confirmed.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] At least two of the four attackers had previously collaborated with the city of Novi Sad in promoting recycling and environmental protection, for which they received funds from the city budget.[^cite_note-262]

Later in the day, farmers blocked the regional road near [Bresnica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bresnica,_%C4%8Ca%C4%8Dak), in central Serbia, with tractors. They announced they will stay there for 24 hours.[^cite_note-263] Another group of farmers blocked the [Ibar Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibar_Highway) near Kraljevo with tractors, for the next 24 hours.[^cite_note-264] In the evening hours, in Novi Sad, Belgrade, Čačak, Kragujevac, Niš and Pirot, thousands gathered in a march of solidarity with the attacked students.[^cite_note-265] In Niš, students drew graffiti on the local SNS offices and announced a blockade of the central ringroad.[^cite_note-266]

Following Vučević's resignation, president Vučić said he would pardon students and university teachers charged in connection with the protests and announced a major cabinet reshuffle, adding that he was considering on whether to call parliamentary elections or appoint a new government.[^cite_note-267] Among those pardoned was the dean of the Faculty of Philology in Niš, who was never informed of any legal proceeding against her.[^cite_note-268]

On 29 January, thousands of citizens protested in [Gornji Milanovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gornji_Milanovac) and hundreds gathered in [Lapovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapovo).[^cite_note-269] In [Ivanjica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivanjica), high-school pupils and their teachers organized a protest, starting with 15 minutes of silence. The school principal brought food for pupils who want to come back to classes, however, the pupils gave the food to [stray dogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stray_dogs), filmed it and shared the video.[^cite_note-270]

### 1 February Novi Sad protest

Also on 29 January, Belgrade students announced that they will walk by foot from Belgrade to Novi Sad to join the blockade on 1 February.[^cite_note-271] High school pupils from the Belgrade municipality of [Zemun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zemun) announced a 24-hour blockade of the [Zemun Gymnasium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zemun_Gymnasium).[^cite_note-272]

On the same day, the magazine *Nova Ekonomija* published an article revealing that the head of president Vučić's cabinet, Ivica Kojić, helped design the reconstruction and adaptation of the railway to Hungary, including the Novi Sad railway station. According to emails published by the Higher Public Prosecutor's Office in Novi Sad, Kojić promised the contractor that he would speed up the project's construction.[^cite_note-273]

On 30 January, hundreds of students from Belgrade universities set off on a two-day walk from Belgrade to Novi Sad in order to support their Novi Sad colleagues in blocking all three bridges in that city, thereby marking three months since the collapse of the canopy. The Ministry of Internal Affairs announced that it would secure the walk route.[^cite_note-274] Along the route, the students were greeted by citizens and by employees of [Telekom Srbija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telekom_Srbija), the state-owned telecommunications company.[^cite_note-275] Citizens of [Stara Pazova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stara_Pazova), a town along their route, welcomed the students with fireworks and over 150 kilograms of free food.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] Before students reached [Inđija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In%C4%91ija), the town's mayor, SNS member Marko Gašić, locked the entrance to the hall where the students were planning to sleep.[^cite_note-278] Residents then offered them private accommodations, including one club owner who offered his night club for the students to sleep in. The students decided to stick together and spent the night in the open, sleeping on styrofoam.[^cite_note-279] In the morning, they proceeded to Novi Sad.[^cite_note-280]

Citizens protested in [Grdelica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grdelica),[^cite_note-281] [Kostolac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kostolac)[^cite_note-282] and in [Pećinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pe%C4%87inci), despite rumors circulating that they would be fired from their jobs.[^cite_note-283] On the same day, the [European Commission](9974.md) officially demanded information from the prosecutors' offices in Belgrade and Novi Sad regarding the attacks on students throughout 2024 and 2025.[^cite_note-284]

On 31 January, another attack with a motor vehicle happened in Belgrade. Medical workers were blocking the street in front of their workplace at the central [psychiatric hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychiatric_hospital), commemorating the victims with 15 minutes of silence, when a black [Volkswagen Golf Mk5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen_Golf_Mk5) drove right through them in full speed. Three medical workers were injured. The perpetrators, two young males, tried to escape, but were stopped by other medical workers.[^cite_note-285]

In the evening hours, marching students reached Novi Sad, where they were welcomed by thousands of local residents. Together, they held a vigil at the Novi Sad railway station, with 15 minutes of silence.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] Over a hundred taxi drivers from Belgrade announced a departure on 2 February at 1 pm from [Nikola Tesla Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Tesla_Airport), in order to transport students back from Novi Sad free of charge.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290]

In Belgrade, employees of the public transit company [GSP Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSP_Belgrade) announced a protest for 5 February, demanding that the mayor, [Aleksandar Šapić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandar_%C5%A0api%C4%87), "be held criminally liable for the damage caused to GSP, together with his assistant Miroslav Čučković".[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292]

On 1 February, on the City Day and the three-month anniversary of the canopy collapse, large crowds gathered at the entrance to Novi Sad; columns of cars waited for hours at the toll gates to join the protests and blockade. A long line of several hundred bikers was seen at the entrance to Novi Sad, and many farmers also joined the protest with their tractors.[^cite_note-293] Around 3 pm, students blocked all three bridges in Novi Sad. Tens of thousands of citizens gathered, and the protest started with 15 minutes of silence. Two of the bridges were blocked for three hours, while [Liberty Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Bridge,_Novi_Sad) was blocked for 24 hours.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] After the students on the bridge voted, it was extended for three more hours in order to clean up the area.[^cite_note-296]

In Čačak, teachers, professors and their students blocked the two bridges over the [West Morava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Morava) for an hour in support of the students in Novi Sad.[^cite_note-297] Farmers have arranged for their tractors to be parked in front of the bridges, in order to protect the protesters.[^cite_note-298] Citizens also protested in [Kladovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kladovo), [Krupanj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krupanj), [Kuršumlija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kur%C5%A1umlija), [Prijepolje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prijepolje), [Prokuplje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prokuplje) and [Žitorađa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDitora%C4%91a).[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] Judges of the Basic and Higher Courts in Niš publicly supported the students' demands.[^cite_note-301] In its [prime-time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime-time) news program, RTS briefly reported about the Novi Sad protest. This was condemned as "scandalous" and "biased" by the SNS.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303]

On 2 February, the Bar Association of Serbia announced a 30-day strike.[^cite_note-N1-2025f-304] In Novi Sad, 671 taxi vehicles from Belgrade organized a lift back to Belgrade for students completely free of charge.[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] On the same day, protests were organized in [Bajina Bašta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajina_Ba%C5%A1ta), [Jagodina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagodina), [Kosjerić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosjeri%C4%87), [Šabac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0abac), [Užice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C5%BEice), [Vlasotince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlasotince) and [Vršac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vr%C5%A1ac).[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] In Šabac, students and teachers blocked the bridge over the [Sava](587845.md) river for 225 minutes, 15 minutes for each of the 15 victims.[^cite_note-309] In Užice, residents blocked the main motorway leading into the city by standing on the bridge above the [Đetinja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%90etinja) river despite freezing temperatures.[^cite_note-310]

### Start of the corruption probe

On 3 February, the Higher Public Prosecutor's Office in Novi Sad announced that the Special Department for Suppression of Corruption had launched an investigation into corruption in connection with the reconstruction of the canopy of the Novi Sad Railway Station.[^cite_note-311] In Belgrade, a 15-minute silence was held at the intersection in front of the city's mental hospital for those killed in the collapse. In addition to employees and citizens, bikers also blocked the intersection in front of the building to protect employees and the crowd, given that two doctors were injured at the same location a few days ago when they were hit by a car at full speed.[^cite_note-312] Students from several Belgrade high schools also protested in front of their respective schools.[^cite_note-313] Artists protested in front of the Ministry of Culture,[^cite_note-314] while professors of the Aviation Academy and the parents whose children attend the academy blocked the intersection in front of the central government building.[^cite_note-315]

On 4 February, students, teachers, farmers and bikers organized a large protest in Kragujevac.[^cite_note-316] The protesters carried boxes with ideas on city improvement to the city hall.[^cite_note-317] Protesters also gathered in [Arandjelovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arandjelovac), for the third time in a week, to support of the kindergarten teachers on strike.[^cite_note-318] On the same day, the Police Union of Serbia (PSS) sent a letter to the Minister of the Interior, [Ivica Dačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivica_Da%C4%8Di%C4%87), demanding an increase in salaries and the introduction of additional benefits for employees in the Ministry of the Interior (MUP). The union proposed that the wages of employees with secondary education, civil servants and office workers be increased by at least 15 percent of the salary for March, while the wages of employees with higher education by at least five percent. In addition, the PSS requested the payment of compensation for food costs during work in the amount of 15,000 dinars for all employees, as well as annual vacation allowance of at least 85,000 dinars, starting in 2025.[^cite_note-319] Nearly 500 policemen have signed a petition supporting the students' demands.[^cite_note-320]

On 5 February, five simultaneous protests were held in Belgrade. Pensioners protested at [Republic Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_Square_\(Belgrade\)) in support of the students.[^cite_note-321] medical workers protested in front of the [Zemun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zemun) hospital,[^cite_note-Nova-2025a-322] students gathered in front of the Ministry of Education,[^cite_note-Nova-2025a-322] another group of students blocked the central street in [Banovo Brdo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banovo_Brdo),[^cite_note-323] and employees of the public transit company GSP protested in front of the company headquarters in [Dorćol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dor%C4%87ol).[^cite_note-324] Students announced a large protest to be held on 15 February in Kragujevac, on the anniversary of the [Sretenje Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sretenje_Constitution).[^cite_note-325] Medical and social workers union "Nezavisnost", with around 2,500 members in Niš, officially supported the students and their demands.[^cite_note-326] Professors and teachers protested in Leskovac.[^cite_note-327] Citizens also protested in [Sombor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sombor), where students were joined by medical workers and local librarians.[^cite_note-328]

On the same day, *Forbes Serbia* published the statement that the former Minister of Construction, [Goran Vesić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goran_Vesi%C4%87), gave to the prosecutor on 22 November 2024. Vesić stated that he does not consider himself responsible, nor could he in any way contribute to the accident, as well as that "the station was not put into operation just because he came to the opening." He denied that the vestibule (main hall) of the station was commissioned on his order. Vesić mentioned that in March 2022, the first part of the station was put into operation, i.e. the platforms and tracks and the part of the station that passengers used until the vestibule was put into operation. And then a ceremony was organized. "At that time, there was no use permit" the statement reads. The statement also describes the procedure for the internal acceptance and handover of Wing B in June–July 2024, from the [Chinese](5405.md) consortium as a contractor to the [Serbian Railways Infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Railways_Infrastructure) as an investor. Vesić said that he found out about the memo, which stated that after the internal reception, passengers will be able to use lobby B, only after the canopy fell. Describing the process of internal reception in more detail, the former minister said that his ministry was not mentioned anywhere in the mutual communication regarding the handover, and that his assistant Anita Dimoski was familiar with the correspondence because she was always named as the recipient of the emails. Concluding the description of the events surrounding the handover, he stated that the handover took place on July 4, 2024, and that the use of the lobby was enabled from that day.[^cite_note-329]

[Slovenian](27338.md) newspapers *[Večer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ve%C4%8Der_\(Slovenia\))* and *[Delo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delo_\(newspaper\))* published speculations that Goran Vesić and Belgrade mayor [Aleksandar Šapić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandar_%C5%A0api%C4%87) had both left Serbia and were currently residing in privately owned mansions in [Trieste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trieste), [Italy](14532.md).[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331]

### Fulfillment of the education funding demand

On 6 February, the [Government of Serbia](608371.md) adopted a decision providing 12.01 billion dinars for higher education. According to the official announcement by the Government, the budget for education is thereby increased by 20 percent, fulfilling the fourth demand of the student plenums.[^cite_note-332] On the same day, in the Novi Sad neighborhood of [Telep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telep), a car with Croatian license plates attempted to drive through the protesters blocking the road for 15 minutes. There were no casualties and the driver fled before police arrived.[^cite_note-333] Parents whose children attend elementary schools in Novi Sad organized a protest after some parents who openly supported the school teachers' strike received phone calls from police.[^cite_note-334] In [Topola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topola), farmers blocked the motorway with tractors.[^cite_note-335] Students also protested in [Crna Trava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crna_Trava).[^cite_note-336]

The Serbian Medical Association (SLD), the umbrella organization of doctors and dentists, announced that their Assembly officially supports all demands issued by the students of the blocked Medical Faculties.[^cite_note-337]

Serbian newspaper *[Danas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danas_\(newspaper\))* published an article revealing that Marko Tošin, one of the attackers who stormed out of the SNS offices in Novi Sad on 28 January and broke a student's jaw with a baseball bat, was released from custody merely a day after the arrest. The article sparked outrage on social media.[^cite_note-Danas-Tosin-7]

According to *Forbes Serbia*, the families of the victims of the Novi Sad accident were interested in joining the criminal prosecution by pressing charges against the persons responsible for the death of their loved ones. This followed testimony before the prosecutor that was attached as part of the indictment, which *Forbes Serbia* had access to.[^cite_note-338] Former dean and professor of the Belgrade Faculty of Civil Engineering, Vladan Kuzmanović, stated that Aleksandar Vučić and Miloš Vučević essentially led the Railway Station reconstruction in Novi Sad.[^cite_note-339] The [European Parliament](9581.md)'s rapporteur for Serbia, [Tonino Picula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonino_Picula), assessed that Serbia is in an "undeclared state of emergency", and that the President Vučić has not found a way to compensate for the dissatisfaction of those people who protest.[^cite_note-340]

On 7 February, lawyers organized a protest in front of the RTS building.[^cite_note-341] The lawyers demanded that their voices be heard and called upon policemen and members of the [Serbian Armed Forces](381170.md) to join a general strike.[^cite_note-342] The informal community of elementary school teachers in [New Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Belgrade) - PULS announced a large protest gathering and walk through New Belgrade on 8 February.[^cite_note-343] Theatrical actors from the theaters in Belgrade, Novi Sad, [Sombor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sombor) and [Zrenjanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zrenjanin) started a seven-day strike.[^cite_note-344] In Belgrade, protesters blocked the [Pupin Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pupin_Bridge) connecting [Zemun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zemun) with [Borča](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bor%C4%8Da).[^cite_note-345] Citizens also protested in Niš.[^cite_note-346] During the protest, an unknown person approached the dean of the Niš Faculty of Philosophy, threatened her and physically attacked her.[^cite_note-347] In [Prijepolje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prijepolje), high school teachers protested, despite receiving verbal threats.[^cite_note-348] In [Subotica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subotica), students, professors and citizens organized a protest, which began with 15 minutes of silence. Afterwards, the gathered crowd moved towards the city hall, where they left a message for the mayor, Stevan Bakić,[^cite_note-349] an SNS member, who previously compared the protesters with[Ustase members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_fascists) and accused them of "destroying Serbia".[^cite_note-350]

In [Bogatić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogati%C4%87), SNS-led local authorities organized a public hearing on the municipal spatial plan, which also includes [lithium](17561.md) exploration, behind closed doors, contrary to the Serbian Law on Local Administration. Hundreds of farmers and protesters entered the Bogatić municipality building, where a public hearing was scheduled to take place, and physically carried the local chairman of the SNS outside. SNS banners, visible on the town square, were torn down.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] Farmers and protesters organized a [plenary session](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plenary_session), during which they expressed support for the students' demands.[^cite_note-354] Kragujevac students from blocked faculties announced they will run a 137-kilometer [relay marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relay_race#Long-distance_relays) to the [Church of St. Sava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Sava) in Belgrade to hand over invitations to their colleagues for the protest scheduled on [Sretenje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sretenje), 15 February, in Kragujevac.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356]

On 8 February, teachers and professors from 18 [New Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Belgrade) elementary schools, joined by parents whose children attend those schools, blocked the central turnaround in New Belgrade.[^cite_note-357] In Niš, students, professors and citizens organized a 15 km-long protest walk to [Merošina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mero%C5%A1ina).[^cite_note-358] On the same day, during his speech at the SNS gathering in a sports hall in Kikinda, president Vučić announced that he is writing a book about his "glorious victory against the [colour revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colour_revolution) in Serbia" and that the book will be finished by [Vidovdan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidovdan).[^cite_note-359] Another SNS gathering was organized in the cultural center of Lazarevac, where President of the National Assembly [Ana Brnabić](54310335.md) and Minister of Finance [Siniša Mali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sini%C5%A1a_Mali), both SNS members, were scheduled to speak. Protesters gathered in front of the center, waited for Brnabić and Mali to come out and [booed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booed) them; during the gathering, Brnabić called the protesters "cowards", but once outside, she smiled and waved at them.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361]

On 9 February, students of the Agronomic Faculty, together with farmers on tractors, blocked the [Gazela Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gazela_Bridge) in Belgrade to commemorate 100 days since the canopy collapse.[^cite_note-362] In Niš, students blocked the highway tolls.[^cite_note-363] Protesters also blocked the local intersections in [Ruma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruma)[^cite_note-364] and [Zrenjanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zrenjanin).[^cite_note-365] In the afternoon hours, students running a relay marathon from Kragujevac to Belgrade, reached [Mladenovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mladenovac), where over a hundred locals gathered along their route to applaud and cheer them on.[^cite_note-366] When students reached the Belgrade municipality of [Voždovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vo%C5%BEdovac), residents formed a column with their vehicles and escorted the students to the [Church of St. Sava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Sava) in downtown Belgrade, where thousands of people gathered to welcome them. The students thanked the crowd, and invited them to come to the large protest scheduled for 15 February in Kragujevac.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368] On the same day, former prime minister Vučević, when asked about the protesters in an interview, said: "Enough with their nonsense. Who gave them the right to determine who is what and who did what, who are they to talk about it? They have no empathy for anything."[^cite_note-369]

On 10 February, a group of students who had been running from Novi Sad to Belgrade since the morning arrived at the plateau in front of the Belgrade Faculty of Philosophy, where a red carpet was laid out for them. They cut the red ribbon holding their index cards and were then awarded medals, with the audience chanting: "Novi Sad, Novi Sad". They were greeted with fireworks as they crossed [Branko's Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branko's_Bridge). The Novi Sad "marathoners" symbolically handed over the [Constitution of Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Serbia) as a baton to their colleagues - students from Belgrade faculties. Participants in the relay marathon from Novi Sad faculties and colleges plan to walk with students blocking Belgrade faculties to Kragujevac.[^cite_note-370]

On 11 February, in temperatures of minus six degrees [Celsius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celsius), 180 students from Niš set off on foot to Kragujevac, where the large protest is scheduled for 15 February.[^cite_note-371] Around 6 pm they arrived in [Aleksinac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksinac), where they spent the night in the local Sports Hall. Along the way, they received applause, chants of support, and a warm welcome was given to them by the villagers in Tešica, who also prepared lunch for them. On the way to Aleksinac, the villagers invited the students to rest for the night, offered them food and refreshments. The students were welcomed in Aleksinac - a banner saying "Welcome, dear students" was placed at the entrance to the promenade, and the students were greeted with fireworks and lots of food and beverages.[^cite_note-372] Students who walked from Novi Sad and Belgrade, arrived in [Sopot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sopot,_Belgrade) in the evening hours. They had planned to sleep in the local sports hall with heating, however, the President of the Municipality Živorad Milosavljević, a member of the SNS, denied permission. The sports hall remained locked, but the citizens organized themselves and found alternative accommodation for the students, in some cases even in plastic [greenhouses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouses).[^cite_note-373] On the same day, Members of the [European Parliament](9581.md) (EP) discussed the situation in Serbia and advised the Serbian authorities that everyone must have the right to freedom of assembly and demanded that all incidents against students and citizens demonstrating in Serbia are investigated. The debate was held at the request of the Social Democrats, and the majority of MEPs supported the protests, saying "Serbian students are the voice of hope and awareness of the entire Balkans".[^cite_note-374]

### Arrest of Milorad Grčić and signs of discontent within SNS

On 12 February, farmers from [Rača](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C4%8Da,_Serbia) blocked all access roads to the municipality with tractors, protesting the increase in local property taxes and in support of students.[^cite_note-375] On the same day, the [Public Attorney's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Attorney's_Office_of_the_Republic_of_Serbia) announced that part of the investigation into the fall of the canopy was transferred to the Prosecutor's Office for Organized Crime.[^cite_note-376] Simultaneously, Milorad Grčić, the former acting director of [Elektroprivreda Srbije](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elektroprivreda_Srbije) (EPS), President of the Belgrade municipality of [Obrenovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obrenovac) and a member of SNS, was arrested, under suspicion that he defrauded EPS for more than one million euros, according to the Higher Public Prosecutor's Office.[^cite_note-377] The government announced a large pro-SNS gathering to be held in [Sremska Mitrovica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sremska_Mitrovica), and started contacting party members and sympathizers. According to [N1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N1_\(TV_channel\)), the organizers of the rally resorted to pressuring employees in public enterprises to come due to decreased support of the government.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379]

On 13 February, students from [Kraljevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraljevo), together with their colleagues from [Novi Pazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novi_Pazar), set off on foot to Kragujevac, where a large gathering was to be held on 15 February. The flags of Kraljevo and Novi Pazar came together in front of the Faculty of Mechanical and Civil Engineering in Kraljevo and the students set off on a walking route of over 50 kilometers. Along the way, they met in [Mrčajevci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mr%C4%8Dajevci) with their colleagues from Užice, Čačak and Gornji Milanovac, from where they continued together to Kragujevac.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] On the same day, the informal community of Belgrade primary schools "PULS" organized a protest in front of the Ministry of Education. They said that they will not negotiate with the Ministry, and informed the public that 65% of schools have suspended classes.[^cite_note-382] Employees of [Radio Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Belgrade) also organized a protest.[^cite_note-383] The [Belgrade Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgrade_Philharmonic_Orchestra) announced it will hold a charity concert, and all proceeds from ticket sales will go to the persons injured by the canopy collapse in Novi Sad.[^cite_note-384]

After news spread about the arrest of the President of Obrenovac, Milorad Grčić, the President of the Assembly of Serbia, [Ana Brnabić](54310335.md), herself head of the Belgrade section of the SNS, came to visit the municipal SNS committee in Obrenovac. In front of the party premises, she was met by a hundred SNS members furious about Grčić's arrest. The attendees refused to allow Brnabić to speak, marking the first publicly visible sign of discontent within the party.[^cite_note-385] According to the weekly magazine *Radar*, in late December, the leaders of all of Serbia's security services had separate meetings with then-prime minister Vučević, where they were supposed to confirm their readiness to "protect the country's constitutional order by any means necessary". Although the surveyed security people answered the question positively, some felt the need to further demonstrate their loyalty in the following days, whereas others were merely thanked by the authorities for their work so far and retired in almost absolute silence.[^cite_note-386]

On 14 February, the Public Prosecutor's Office in Belgrade proposed [suspended sentences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suspended_sentences) for the persons who physically attacked the students of the Belgrade Faculty of Law.[^cite_note-387] On the same day, during his official visit to the [Republika Srpska](347422.md), president Vučić compared the protesters in Serbia with "sediment and filth which occasionally rises to the surface".[^cite_note-388] The Serbian subsidiary of [Assicurazioni Generali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assicurazioni_Generali) decided to withdraw from the work of the [Serbian Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Chamber_of_Commerce) and the Association of Insurers of Serbia, after both organizations refused to respond to tabloid attacks that the president of the company's executive board, Dragan Filipović, was subjected to. The pro-government tabloids *Informer* and *Republika* published a photo of Filipović at the student protests which he attended in a private capacity, accusing him of "wanting to destroy the country". The company itself was also targeted, through texts that unfoundedly claimed that it was losing the Serbian market.[^cite_note-389]

### *Sretnimo se na Sretenje*

On 15 February, the anniversary of the 1835 declaration of the [Sretenje Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sretenje_Constitution), a large protest was held in Kragujevac. The gathering, named "Sretnimo se na Sretenje" ("Let's Meet on Sretenje"), lasted for 15 hours, during which time Lepenički Boulevard, one of the main roads in the city, was blockaded. Many students present at the protest had walked on foot, some traveling as far as 150 kilometers to attend. Large numbers of vehicles also made their way to the city, with a total of 165,000 protesters attending the gathering.[^cite_note-390] Protesters also gathered at Trg Republike in Belgrade in support of the gathering in Kragujevac.[^cite_note-391] President Vučić held a counter-rally that same day in [Sremska Mitrovica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sremska_Mitrovica), at which he described the protests as "the dirtiest color revolution in the history of mankind" and claimed that three billion euros had been invested by foreign powers to topple his government.[^cite_note-392] The executive board of the United Branch Unions "Nezavisnost" ("Independence") stated that public sector employees were pressured to attend the pro-government rally,[^cite_note-393] at which some 20,000 people were present.[^cite_note-394] The next day, around 300 taxi drivers drove to Kragujevac to transport the students back home, where they were greeted warmly.[^cite_note-395] An additional protest was announced for 1 March in Niš.[^cite_note-396]

On 18 February, the farmers who protested in [Rača](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C4%8Da,_Serbia) stormed the local municipality building and occupied it, demanding lower taxes and resignations of local officials.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398] The farmers expressed their support for the student protesters.[^cite_note-399]

On 19 February, the organization CRTA (Center for Research, Transparency and Accountability) released a survey of a representative sample of Serbian citizens' opinions on the protests.[^cite_note-400] According to the report, around 80% of respondents supported the majority of the students' demands and 64% support or mostly support the protests themselves, and that 33% of respondents claimed to have taken part in at least one protest in Serbia during the previous three months. When asked who they would trust more, 46% of respondents stated they would trust the students, 21% would trust President Vučić, 12% would trust both equally, with the remaining 21% trusting neither or having no answer.[^cite_note-401]

On the same day, in Kraljevo, opposition councilors blocked the city assembly. Protests in front of the assembly building would last for several hours, and eggs were thrown at the mayor, Predrag Terzić. One opposition councilor was detained by the police, and the mayor was evacuated in a police vehicle.[^cite_note-402]

On 20 February, a protest was held at [Vukov Spomenik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vukov_Spomenik) in Belgrade, where 15 minutes of silence was observed.[^cite_note-403] The gathering, which was organized by citizens from the municipalities of Stari Grad, Savski Venac, Palilula, Zvezdara, and Vračar, as well as by the organization "Ustala je Ustanička,"[^cite_note-404] was attended by nearly 14,000 protesters.[^cite_note-405]

On 21 February, students, educators, parents, and citizens in [Prijepolje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prijepolje) held a gathering in memory of the 15 lives lost in Novi Sad. The protest was attended by students of the [State University of Novi Pazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_University_of_Novi_Pazar), at the request of whom the 15 minutes of silence were extended to 16 in remembrance of Ernad Bakan,[^cite_note-406] a student from Novi Pazar killed in 2019 while studying in Belgrade by a speeding car. Protesters considered the sentence given to the driver as lenient.[^cite_note-Vreme-Novi_Pazar-197] Additional protests were held that day in [Čačak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%8Ca%C4%8Dak), [Ivanjica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivanjica), and [Ljig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljig). During the 12-hour blockade in Čačak, a plenum of students and citizens voted to boycott RTS and announced they would walk on foot to attend the gathering in Niš on 1 March. Students of Mihajlo Pupin Technical Department in [Zrenjanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zrenjanin), who had left the city the day before with the goal of walking to [Vršac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vr%C5%A1ac), were greeted warmly in the villages of [Velika Greda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velika_Greda) and later in [Plandište](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plandi%C5%A1te), where they stayed the night.[^cite_note-407]

On 24 February, Žarko Mićin was elected as the new mayor of Novi Sad by the City Assembly, with 45 councilors voting in his favor. The opposition boycotted the session, citing a heavy police and [Žandarmerija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDandarmerija) presence around the assembly building, which led to clashes with citizens protesting the election process. Protesters attempted to enter the assembly and threw eggs, red paint, yogurt, flour, and toilet paper at the police and the building, prompting multiple police interventions to prevent them from approaching the premises. The police reported that five officers were injured during the protests and denied allegations that opposition councilors were prevented from attending the session. In his address, Mićin emphasized his commitment to being a mayor for all citizens of Novi Sad and prioritized the inspection of all city-owned buildings to prevent future tragedies.[^cite_note-408] On the same day, SNS organized a gathering in [Srbobran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srbobran) in a local theater, attended by the resigned Prime Minister [Miloš Vučević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milo%C5%A1_Vu%C4%8Devi%C4%87). The citizens organized a protest in front of the venue and [booed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booed) at Vučević.[^cite_note-409] In the village of Cvetke, near Kraljevo, the gathered villagers threw eggs at the visiting mayor of Kraljevo, Predrag Terzić.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411]

On 25 February, the Parliament Speaker and head of the Belgrade branch of SNS, [Ana Brnabić](54310335.md), told the media that the [Serbian National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_National_Assembly) will consider the resignation of Prime Minister Miloš Vučević as the last item on the agenda at its session on 4 March.[^cite_note-412]

On 27 February, pupils of the Fifth Belgrade Gymnasium blocked entry into their school, protesting the appointment of the new, SNS-affiliated principal.[^cite_note-413] President Vučić commented on the event by saying: "The terror that you see here is being carried out by the pupils of the Fifth Belgrade Gymnasium against the school principal Danka Nešović."[^cite_note-414]

On 28 February, seven archbishops of the [Serbian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church) signed an open letter supporting the students' right to protest and condemning the derogatory speech aimed against them. "As hierarchs of the Serbian Orthodox Church, we feel a duty to publicly express our disagreement and distance ourselves from any speech that dehumanizes another person, and especially from speech that belittles the young people, students, as a factor in building the society, a factor that contributes to its future, or that humiliates them as the ones called upon to think and seek the truth."[^cite_note-415]

### Student Edict
